# 10. Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Build a RACI matrix and onboarding workflow for data-science teams
---
## Executive definition
Data scientists own experiments, data preparation, model code, evaluation, and documented requirements; platform administrators own the secure and reliable shared environment, policies, capacity, integrations, and operational guardrails.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Eliminate role ambiguity.
- Create standard onboarding.
- Define escalation paths.
- Separate user and platform controls.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Raci
- Define what **RACI** means for Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for RACI.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for RACI.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for RACI, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for RACI.
- Review how RACI changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: projects
- **Purpose:** Describe why projects is relevant to Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by projects.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by projects.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by projects.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for projects.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for projects.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for projects.

## Enterprise architecture layers
### Layer 1: Business Outcome And Product Management
1. State the responsibility of the business outcome and product management layer for Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities.
2. Identify authoritative configuration sources for the business outcome and product management layer.
3. List upstream and downstream dependencies of the business outcome and product management layer.
4. Define isolation boundaries and data classification at the business outcome and product management layer.
5. Define SLIs for availability, latency, throughput, quality, saturation, and errors where applicable.
6. Define support ownership and escalation from project team to platform team to vendor.
7. Capture evidence through logs, events, metrics, traces, manifests, approvals, and test results.
8. Design rollback and disaster-recovery behavior for failures at the business outcome and product management layer.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "data-scientist-vs-platform-admin"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: data-scientist-vs-platform-admin
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/data-scientist-vs-platform-admin
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Build a RACI matrix and onboarding workflow for data-science teams
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for projects, with explicit consideration of RACI.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for workbenches, with explicit consideration of self-service boundaries.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for connections, with explicit consideration of workspace management.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for PVCs, with explicit consideration of data access.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for images, with explicit consideration of image governance.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for hardware profiles, with explicit consideration of resource requests.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for pipelines, with explicit consideration of deployment handoff.
8. Define validation commands and expected results for model deployments, with explicit consideration of incident ownership.
9. Document failure modes and rollback for RBAC, with explicit consideration of RACI.
10. Review capacity, cost, and licensing impact for quota, with explicit consideration of self-service boundaries.
11. Peer-review the design with platform and application teams for monitoring, with explicit consideration of workspace management.
12. Obtain change approval before production for support process, with explicit consideration of data access.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, using projects as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate RACI, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate self-service boundaries, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate workspace management, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate data access, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate image governance, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate resource requests, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate deployment handoff, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate incident ownership, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The projects state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to self-service boundaries.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities must implement documented identity and access controls for projects.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for workspace management, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities and is first observed around images.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass self-service boundaries controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities and is first observed around quota.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass workspace management controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 3: Workbench Remains Pending
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities and is first observed around connections.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Data distribution or quality shift.
- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass data access controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 4: Pvc Does Not Bind
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities and is first observed around model deployments.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Namespace or project mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get datasciencecluster -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc logs <pod> -n <namespace> --all-containers`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass image governance controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **First command:** `oc get dscinitialization -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc get resourcequota,limitrange -A`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate AI platform to projects, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate MLOps to workbenches, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate GenAIOps to connections, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate ModelOps to PVCs, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate Workbench to images, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities, relate Pipeline to hardware profiles, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **Data scientist vs platform admin responsibilities** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
